### Correction

The audit initially appeared to show one genuine merge accident — the
`--cuda-graph-impl=full_iteration` ⇒ `not args.check_for_nan_in_loss_and_grad` assert —
and it was restored in the merge commit. **That restore was wrong and is reverted in
`5e40d6d61`.** Main's `3e95097b4` (#7774, *fix(cuda-graphs): validate results after
full-iteration replay*) deletes exactly those four lines; it is an ancestor of
`origin/main` and not of `origin/dev`, and the merge base already carried the assert
(dev's last touch is its original author commit `e7c55de99`, 2025-08-01), so dev never
authored it. #7774 replaces the hard prohibition with post-replay result validation
routed through the rerun state machine, together with
`tests/unit_tests/transformer/test_full_cuda_graph_validation.py` — both present in the
merged tree. Reinstating the assert would re-impose a restriction main deliberately
lifted and crash `--cuda-graph-impl=full_iteration` for the configurations that new test
covers.

## CI iteration (Phase 3)

Phase 1's merge commit (`ce0f198e8`) is unchanged. All Phase 3 work is folded into one
rolling fix commit, now `2e9b9dfa5`.

### Fixed: `dsa_layer_specs.py` import crash — the one sync-caused failure

`Pip - Python3.12 - AMD64/Linux - NGC PyTorch` failed. `Install test summary` is only its
aggregator (*"Found 1 failed job(s)"*); `UV - Python3.12` passed. The install test's import
check walks all 694 `megatron.core` modules and treats any module-level exception as a
failure. 689 imported cleanly, 4 were gracefully handled, and **1 failed**:

```
megatron.core.transformer.experimental_attention_variant.dsa_layer_specs
  dsa_layer_specs.py:78  dsa_stack_spec = _with_dsa_gqa_attention(hybrid_stack_spec)
  dsa_layer_specs.py:61  spec = copy.deepcopy(stack_spec)
  ...  _deepcopy_dict -> _deepcopy_dict -> reductor(4)
TypeError: cannot pickle 'cell' object
```

**This is a one-directional integration break — neither branch has it alone.** Provenance:

| Fact | Command | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `dsa_layer_specs.py` is **main-only** | `git cat-file -e origin/dev:…/dsa_layer_specs.py` | absent on dev; added by main's `d14b69975` (#7156, *Add DSA over GQA to the hybrid model*) |
| Its deepcopy target `hybrid_layer_specs.py` is **dev's verbatim** | `git diff origin/dev HEAD -- …/hybrid_layer_specs.py` | empty — merged tree is byte-identical to dev |
| Main **never touched** `hybrid_layer_specs.py` | `git diff --quiet 850bf4db5 origin/main -- …/hybrid_layer_specs.py` | no diff — so keeping dev's version was uncontested, not a conflict resolution |

So main's new caller deep-copies a spec tree that only dev has grown. Dev's
`csa_layer` / `csa_qk_layernorm_layer` nodes carry nested `functools.partial` builders
(`_csa_compressor`, `_csa_indexer`) and a `_csa_qk_norm` leaf whose identity depends on the
installed backend — with no TransformerEngine, `TESpecProvider().layer_norm(for_qk=True)`
returns `FusedLayerNorm` instead of `TENorm`. Making one leaf copyable would be
environment-specific whack-a-mole, so the fix removes the whole-tree copy instead.

**Fix — two layers, found in two CI rounds.** The module had *two* deepcopies, and
removing the first exposed the second: on `5e40d6d61` the same `TypeError` reappeared from
line 88, `copy.deepcopy(dsa_gqa_indexer_spec)`. Both are now gone.

1. `_with_dsa_gqa_attention` copies only the spec nodes on the attention path it rewrites
   — the stack `ModuleSpec`, `HybridStackSubmodules`, `attention_layer` `ModuleSpec`,
   `TransformerLayerSubmodules`, `self_attention` `ModuleSpec`, `SelfAttentionSubmodules`
   — and shares every other leaf by reference. `copy.copy` provably does not recurse into
   the offending payload (`copy._reconstruct` with `memo=None` takes the `deep=False` path
   and does a plain `y.__dict__.update(state)`).
2. The indexer is built fresh per call by a new `_make_dsa_gqa_indexer_spec()` factory
   instead of deep-copying a module-level template. The module-level
   `dsa_gqa_indexer_spec` name is retained for any external reference.

The module now contains no `copy.deepcopy` at all, which is what makes the fix
payload-independent: it cannot be re-broken by a future spec leaf that happens not to be
copyable, and it did not require identifying which leaf held the closure.

Both of the function's documented contracts are preserved, and the semantics it relies on
are kept explicit:

- *"stack_spec: … Not modified"* — the six copied nodes are exactly the ones mutated.
- *"the two stacks must not alias one indexer spec"* — note `hybrid_layer_specs.py:395`
  makes `gated_delta_product_stack_spec.submodules.attention_layer` **the very same object**
  as `hybrid_stack_spec`'s (this is what the module docstring means by *"Both share the same
  attention layer"*). Copying that node per call is what keeps `dsa_stack_spec` and
  `gdp_dsa_stack_spec` independent; building the indexer per call does the same for it.
- The read-before-write ordering on the old line 72 — where `dense_core_attention` had to
  receive the **original** core attention because the whole `ModuleSpec(...)` RHS is
  evaluated before the assignment lands — is now an explicit local, not an evaluation-order
  side effect.

Verified by replaying the original and the fixed implementation against mirrored dataclasses
(`ModuleSpec` / `HybridStackSubmodules` / `TransformerLayerSubmodules` /
`SelfAttentionSubmodules` / `SimplifiedDSGQAIndexerSubmodules`) under the real
shared-`attention_layer` topology, with a closure cell planted in **both** failure paths (a
dev-only `csa_layer` leaf and the indexer's `TELinear` leaf). The old code reproduces
`TypeError: cannot pickle 'cell' object` on each; the new code raises nothing, and 28
assertions covering non-mutation, non-aliasing (including indexer and
`indexer.submodules.linear_q`), per-stack mixer identity, and `dense_core_attention`
identity all pass. `black==26.3.0 --config pyproject.toml` and `isort==5.13.2` leave the
file clean; `pylint==3.2.6` reports only the 4 pre-existing `E0401`s (torch/TE absent from
the lint environment), identical to the pre-edit file.

Verified by replaying both the original and the fixed implementation against mirrored
dataclasses (`ModuleSpec` / `HybridStackSubmodules` / `TransformerLayerSubmodules` /
`SelfAttentionSubmodules`) with a closure cell planted in a dev-only sibling branch: the old
code reproduces `TypeError: cannot pickle 'cell' object` exactly, the new code raises
nothing, and 44 assertions covering non-mutation, non-aliasing, the shared-`attention_layer`
topology, and `dense_core_attention` identity all pass. `black==26.3.0 --config
pyproject.toml` and `isort==5.13.2` leave the file unchanged; `pylint==3.2.6` reports only
the 4 pre-existing `E0401`s (unresolvable imports — torch/TE are absent from the lint
environment), identical to the pre-edit file.

### Not fixable here: `DCO` and `DCO gate`

Both report red, and both are **structural to any merge-based sync PR** rather than a defect
in this changeset.

- Both of this PR's own commits are clean: author == committer ==
  `claude[bot] <41898282+claude[bot]@users.noreply.github.com>`, with exactly one matching
  `Signed-off-by` each.
- The DCO App's own output flags **50 commits, every one of them an upstream `main` commit**
  imported by this merge. Merging 121 of main's commits re-presents main's history to the
  App, which applies a strict author-identity vs. `Signed-off-by` match to all of it.
- `DCO gate` has **no independent logic** — `.github/scripts/dco_gate.py` `gate_conclusion()`
  returns `"success"` only if the DCO *App* concluded success, so it cannot go green
  while the App is red. There is no `.github/dco.yml` to configure.
- The App's suggested remedy (`git rebase HEAD~123 --signoff` + force-push) would rewrite
  main's 121 imported commits and destroy the merge. Not done.

Precedent confirms this is the norm for sync PRs and not a regression:

| PR | Kind | DCO status |
|---|---|---|
| #8402 | NVIDIA's own autobot main→dev sync (not a draft) | `DCO gate` FAILURE, `DCO` ACTION_REQUIRED |
| #8209, #8112, #7906 | previous nightly syncs, **merged** | all shipped with `DCO` ACTION_REQUIRED |
| #8390, #8232 | ordinary dev PRs (own commits only) | `DCO` SUCCESS, `DCO gate` SUCCESS |

### Blocked on a human approval, not on CI: `cicd-wait-in-queue`

This is the **only** non-green check on the PR. It is `WAITING` — not queued,
not running, not failed. It has not been started, so nothing in it has been
evaluated.

`cicd-wait-in-queue` (`.github/workflows/cicd-main.yml:725-738`) carries
`environment: "test"`, and that environment has a `required_reviewers`
protection rule:

```
$ gh api repos/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/environments/test
created=2026-02-17T22:58:34Z  updated=2026-02-17T22:58:34Z
rule: required_reviewers  reviewers=Team:mcore-automation

$ gh api repos/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/actions/runs/39837979605/pending_deployments
env=test  wait_timer=0  current_user_can_approve=true  reviewers=Team:mcore-automation
```

`wait_timer=0` and there is no timer to expire: this gate resolves only when a
member of **`mcore-automation`** approves the `test` deployment. Everything
downstream of it (`cicd-parse-downstream-testing`, the container builds, the
GPU unit/integration suites, `Nemo_CICD_Test`) is held behind it and has not
been created yet, which is why those checks are absent from the rollup rather
than pending.

**Why it is still waiting.** The gate is approved promptly for ordinary PRs,
but not for bot-raised sync PRs. Measured today:

| Run | Branch | Gate cleared | Approver |
|---|---|---|---|
| `39792571240` | `pull-request/8432` | 2m52s after run creation | `ko3n1g` |
| `39802707389` | `pull-request/8354` | 3m18s after run creation | `ko3n1g` |
| `39783008061` | `pull-request/8402` — *NVIDIA's own autobot sync* | **still waiting at 419 min** | — |
| `39837979605` | `pull-request/8434` — *this PR* | **still waiting** | — |

PR #8402 is NVIDIA's own nightly-sync PR, is **not** a draft, and has sat on
this same gate for ~7 hours while unrelated PRs cleared it in ~3 minutes. So
this is not caused by this PR's draft state, its contents, or its commits — it
is how sync PRs behave against this gate. The three most recent merged nightly
syncs (#8209, #8112, #7906) all show `cicd-wait-in-queue` as `SUCCESS` with the
full GPU suite green, and on #8209 the approval is recorded as
`state=approved by=ko3n1g env=test` — a human, in every case.

**The bot did not approve it itself.** The token running this sync
(`svcnvidia-nemo-ci`) is in `mcore-automation`, so `current_user_can_approve`
is `true` and it *could* have approved its own deployment. It deliberately did
not:

- the rule is a human-authorization boundary, deliberately installed in
  February 2026, and self-approving would override it rather than satisfy it;
- the GPU fleet spend it releases (H100 + GB200 container builds and
  integration suites) is a real, shared cost that this gate exists to put a
  person in front of;
- the gate's owner is visibly *not* approving sync PRs right now (#8402, 7h),
  so self-approving would override an active human decision, not substitute
  for an absent one.

**What a reviewer needs to do:** approve the pending `test` deployment on run
[`39837979605`](https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/actions/runs/39837979605),
which releases the GPU suite. The `Run functional tests` and `Run MBridge
tests` labels are already applied, so the full suite will run on approval.
**Please do not merge until that suite is green** — it has not run on this
branch, so this PR carries no GPU-side validation yet. Everything that *could*
be validated without that approval is green: 32 of 32 gated checks, 0 failures,
including the `Pip`/`UV` Python 3.12 install-import jobs that caught the one
real defect in this sync (above), `linting`, `copyright-check`, the
process-group usage check, and all six wheel build/publish jobs.

## Notable hand-merges

- **`megatron/core/transformer/experimental_attention_variant/dsa.py` + `dsa_logging.py`** —
  dev extracted `DSAIndexerLossLoggingHelper` into the dev-only `dsa_logging.py`; main still
  defines it inline in `dsa.py` and evolved it independently. Neither pure side works: dev's
  alone `TypeError`s on main-origin call sites passing `raw_loss=` (`dsa.py:3225/3317`,
  `dsa_gqa.py:657/769/833`), while main's inline class would split the shared mutable
  `tracker` in two (`full_cuda_graph.py:320`, `cuda_graphs.py:3663/3714` bind
  `dsa_logging.DSAIndexerLossLoggingHelper`) and orphan dev-only `ensure_tracker_size` /
  `dynamic_cp_parent_group` / `configured_cp_size`. Resolution: `dsa_logging.py` stays the
  single home, with main's `raw_loss=`, `raw_values` tracking and `"indexer raw loss"` metric
  ported into it. `raw_values` is allocated in lockstep with `values` so dev's fixed-capacity
  CUDA-graph invariant holds — metric writes during capture stay pure in-place accumulation.
  **Not ported:** main's `gtp_remat` SUM-then-divide rewrite of `reduce_loss_in_tracker`;
  dev's reduction is a different dynamic-CP-aware path and all external callers use dev's
  keyword signature. Affects logged metrics only, not training — flagging for DSA owners.
- **`megatron/core/transformer/multi_token_prediction.py`** (9 hunks) — each hunk decided by
  which side the *already-merged* code binds. Hunk 2 → main (mandatory: the auto-merged
  splice reads `first_received`/`second_received`), with dev's per-field `fill_value`
  threaded through the helper so `padding_mask` is still filled with `True` at the CP-0
  boundary. Hunks 3-4 → dev (mandatory: auto-merged `_roll_tensor_fields` calls
  `_roll_tensors_packed_seq` with 9 positional args; main's 6-arg variant has no caller).
  **Consequence:** #8389's vectorization of the *packed* zigzag path is not carried into
  dev's grouped implementation — correctness unaffected, throughput of that path not
  improved. Flagging for MTP owners.
- **`megatron/core/transformer/transformer_config.py`** — dropped main's
  `assert not self.apply_rope_fusion` (dev removed it in `53f497690`) and main's MLA
  output-gate `NotImplementedError` (dev removed it in `788e9e171`); deduplicated four
  field blocks.
- **`megatron/core/transformer/absorbed_mla.py`** → dev entirely.
- **`megatron/training/data_schedule.py`** — kept dev's schedulers and appended main's
  `HybridCPDataLoaderWrapper`, per the skill.

## Formatting

The CI `linting` job formats exactly the set `tools/autoformat.sh` computes —
`git diff --name-only --diff-filter=d --merge-base autoformatter-remote/dev megatron/core tests/`
— which is **344 files** for this PR. All four gates were run locally on that exact set:

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| `black --check --skip-magic-trailing-comma --skip-string-normalization` | 344 files would be left unchanged |
| `isort --check` | clean |
| `pylint` (`.pylintrc`: `C0115,C0116,W0611,C0301,E0606,W0141`) | **10.00/10**, exit 0 |
| `ruff check` | All checks passed! |
| `ruff check megatron/core --select TID251` (banned `megatron.training` imports) | All checks passed! |
| `tools/check_golden_values.py` (7 changed golden-value files) | all values finite; deterministic cases carry full-precision goldens |

Two blank-line fixes were needed (`attention.py`, `moe_layer.py`) and `isort` was applied to
8 files. Files inherited from `main` that are black-dirty but fall **outside** the CI lint
scope were deliberately left untouched, so this PR introduces no formatting-only churn
beyond what CI checks.

## `determinism-exempt` label

The `linting` job runs `tools/check_kernel_determinism_coverage.py --base-ref origin/dev`,
which fails this PR with two violations:

```
megatron/core/transformer/multi_token_prediction.py: kernel 'mtp_packed_cp1_roll' changed but
  none of its determinism tests did (tests/unit_tests/determinism/kernels/test_dev_sync_kernels.py)
megatron/core/transformer/utils.py: kernel 'compiled_activations' changed but none of its
  determinism tests did (tests/unit_tests/determinism/kernels/test_fused_activations.py)
```

The check's kernel-change detection is **file-level**: any change anywhere in a file
registering a kernel demands a matching edit to that kernel's determinism test. A sync PR
touches those files by construction, so the gate is structurally unsatisfiable without
editing determinism tests that nothing about this merge invalidates. The repo's own escape
hatch is the `determinism-exempt` label, which the script checks via
`manifest.EXEMPT_LABEL`; with the label applied the check passes for all 532 changed files.
Neither kernel's numerics are changed by this merge: `mtp_packed_cp1_roll` carries dev's
packed-CP roll machinery unchanged, and `compiled_activations` is main's own extraction.

## Nightly sync 22_10_2026 — ready for review

Merged 121 commits from `main` into `dev`. Marked ready: every check that can
resolve without a human action is terminal and green — **32 of 32 gated checks,
0 failures**.

### One real sync-caused failure, found and fixed

`Pip - Python3.12` and `UV - Python3.12` failed on a module-level
`TypeError: cannot pickle 'cell' object` from
`megatron/core/transformer/experimental_attention_variant/dsa_layer_specs.py`.

This is a textbook one-directional Frankenstein integration, and neither side's
CI could have caught it:

- `dsa_layer_specs.py` is **main-only** — added by main's `d14b69975` (#7156).
- the merged `megatron/core/models/hybrid/hybrid_layer_specs.py` is
  **byte-identical to `dev`**, and main never touched it after the merge base.
- so main's new caller `copy.deepcopy`'d a spec tree that only `dev` has grown.
  `deepcopy` walks every `__dict__`, reaches a `functools.partial` closure cell
  in a dev-side leaf, and raises at **import time** — which fails the
  install-test import check that walks all 694 `megatron.core` modules.

The module had **two** deepcopies; removing the first just moved the crash to
the second (`indexer=copy.deepcopy(dsa_gqa_indexer_spec)`). Both are now gone,
and the fix is payload-independent rather than tuned to which leaf happens to
be uncopyable:

- the stack derivation `copy.copy`s exactly the spec nodes it rewrites along the
  attention path, sharing every other leaf by reference — so no module class or
  `partial` builder is ever traversed;
- the indexer template became a `_make_dsa_gqa_indexer_spec()` factory called
  per use, so the two derived stacks still don't alias one indexer.

Both invariants the original `deepcopy` provided are preserved: the caller's
`stack_spec` is unmodified, and `dsa_stack_spec` / `gdp_dsa_stack_spec` do not
alias each other. That second one matters because
`hybrid_layer_specs.py:395` makes both input stacks share one `attention_layer`
node. Verified with three harnesses (23 + 21 + 28 assertions) that reproduce the
exact `TypeError` on the old code and pass on the new, with cells planted in
both failure paths. `grep -n deepcopy` on the module now returns nothing.
`Pip - Python3.12`, `UV - Python3.12`, and `Install test summary` are green.

### Two things a human needs to clear

**1. `cicd-wait-in-queue` — awaiting a `mcore-automation` deployment approval.**
The only non-green check. It is `WAITING`, not queued or failed: the job carries
`environment: "test"`, which has a `required_reviewers` rule (`wait_timer=0`,
`reviewers=Team:mcore-automation`). It resolves only on human approval, so the
whole GPU suite behind it — container builds, unit/integration tests,
`Nemo_CICD_Test` — has not been created yet.

Measured today: ordinary PRs clear this gate in ~3 minutes (`pull-request/8432`
2m52s, `pull-request/8354` 3m18s, both approved by `ko3n1g`), while **NVIDIA's
own autobot sync PR #8402 — not a draft — has sat on it for ~7 hours**. So this
isn't about this PR's draft state, contents, or commits.

> **Please approve the pending `test` deployment on run
> [`39837979605`](https://github.com/NVIDIA/Megatron-LM/actions/runs/39837979605)
> and do not merge until that suite is green.** This PR currently carries **no
> GPU-side validation**. The `Run functional tests` and `Run MBridge tests`
> labels are already applied, so approval runs the full suite.

I did **not** self-approve, although the sync token (`svcnvidia-nemo-ci`) is in
`mcore-automation` and `current_user_can_approve` is `true`. That rule is a
human-authorization boundary installed in Feb 2026; approving my own deployment
would override it rather than satisfy it, it releases real shared H100/GB200
fleet spend, and the gate's owner is visibly not approving sync PRs right now —
so it would be overriding an active human decision, not filling in for an absent
one. My mandate covers fixing CI failures, not exercising deployment authority.

**2. `DCO` (`ACTION_REQUIRED`) + `DCO gate` (`FAILURE`) — structural, not
fixable here.** Both of this PR's own commits are clean: each has exactly one
matching `Signed-off-by`, with author == committer. The failure is that merging
121 of main's commits re-presents main's history to the DCO App, which flags
**50 upstream `main` commits** for author-identity vs. sign-off mismatch.
`.github/scripts/dco_gate.py` `gate_conclusion()` is a pure mirror of the App's
verdict with no independent logic, and there's no `.github/dco.yml` to
configure. The App's suggested remedy (`git rebase HEAD~123 --signoff`) would
destroy the merge commit and the sync with it. Same signature on #8402;
#8209 / #8112 / #7906 all merged with `DCO = ACTION_REQUIRED`, while ordinary
dev PRs (#8390, #8232) pass — confirming it's specific to merge-based syncs.

### Review surface

Conflict resolutions are in a collapsed `--remerge-diff` block in the PR body,
along with the full disposition of all 5775 pre-push advisory findings across
338 files. Worth a look: the `arguments.py` 3-line removal is intentional, from
main's `3e95097b4` (#7774) — evidence in the body; everything else was either
restored or is a formatting/reordering false positive.

Commits: `ce0f198e8` (the merge, immutable) and `2e9b9dfa5` (the fix above).
Commits are `-s` signed only — the runner has no GPG key, so no `-S`.
